# Social Studies course — CURRICULUM (the path)

Social Studies is one world course, organised by **levels, not
grades**: a learner climbs when they are ready, not when a
school calendar says so. The school grades the books come from
appear only in each lesson's sources (provenance), never as the
course's organisation. The path climbs from the learner's own
ground outward (see TITLES.md): **First Steps → Earth and Space
→ Population, Transport and Maps → People and Resources →
Ethiopia: Ancient and Medieval → Africa to 1500 → The Ancient
World → The Land and Air → Culture and Work → Resources and
Conflict → Modern Ethiopia → Africa after 1500 → The Modern
World → Global Issues**.

**The first page (what learners are told):** everything in this
course comes from government textbooks — this part from
Ethiopia's Social Studies books for Grades 7 and 8 — except the
**Tip boxes**: a tip in a box is from us, the people making
LiqLearns. Tips are ours; the lessons are the books'. See
ABOUT.md for the full book list, the pairing warning (Addis
Ababa guides against Amhara student books), the truncated
Grade 8 guide, and the acceptance reckoning.

## Where the build stands

Building — Ethiopia Wave 3 (Social Studies, Grades 7–8).
Band records land here as each band is built and verified.

- **First Steps + Earth and Space — BUILT** (Grade 7 student
  Chapter 1 + both books' way + teacher guide Unit 1): 16
  lessons, 198 questions, pictures 11000–11040, 11 GIFs (all
  frame-checked). Four guide-only lessons (solar system
  formation, the four spheres, crust materials, rocks in
  Ethiopia) stand on teacher-guide evidence only, counted
  nowhere. The guide's garbled Activity 1.2 rotation key is not
  taught and is disowned in the sources.
- **Population, Transport and Maps — BUILT** (Grade 7 student
  Chapter 2 + teacher guide Unit 2): 19 lessons, 196 questions,
  pictures 11080–11102, GIFs 11088 + 11091 (both frame-checked).
  Two guide-only lessons (human evolution; population measuring
  change) stand on teacher-guide evidence only, counted nowhere.
  The guide's §3.2 optimum-population framework was first built
  here as well; it now stands once, in People and Resources
  (dedup, 2026-11-12). No guide coverage claimed for
  maps/globes/GPS — the guide has none.
- **People and Resources — BUILT** (Grade 7 student
  Chapter 3 + teacher guide Unit 3): 8 lessons, 121 questions,
  pictures 11150–11160, GIFs 11161–11163 (all frame-checked).
  One guide-only lesson (too many, too few, just right — the
  guide's §3.2 optimum-population framework) stands on
  teacher-guide evidence only, counted nowhere; guide parts

## Global Issues (Unit 14) — built 2026-11-12 (Wave 3 Part 2 continuation; picture range 11400–11417)

15 lessons, 206 questions. Grade 7 Chapter 7 + Grade 7 teacher-guide Unit 7 + Grade 8 Chapter 7. Grade 7 sits lower, Grade 8 climbs — inside the same lesson for Water (Grade 7 pollution → Grade 8 shortage and solutions) and via labelled guide evidence in Population Effects, Terrorism and Trafficking. Two guide-only lessons (Global Warming/Ozone/Waste/Biodiversity; Protecting the Environment) carry the 0-countries acceptance line; guide material labelled teacher evidence with the not-edition-matched warning; NO Grade 8 teacher-guide coverage claimed anywhere (the held guide ends at Unit 3). Green-economy dates labelled E.C. throughout. Book errors taught corrected and named in sources (desert list numbering 1,2,2,3; the "2201" caption; the HIV cure line taught as the book's dated framing; the guide §7.2 garbled "overload" definition disowned). Pictures 11400–11401, 11403–11416 (16, drawn PIL, every one looked at — 12 remade for edge-clipping and re-looked); GIFs 11402 (topsoil washing away) and 11417 (coppice shoots sprouting) frame-checked PASS (11417 after a stump redraw and re-check). Assessment placements folded into the prove-it pools by design; reference shelves and guide front matter already stand in ABOUT.md. Build log: ethiopia-2026-11-11/build-logs/ss-G-global-issues.md.

## Modern Ethiopia (Unit 11) — built 2026-11-12 (Wave 3 Part 2 continuation; picture range 11600–11633)

32 lessons, 413 questions — one lesson per Grade 8 student placement 77–108 (Chapter 4, printed pp. 102–141), story-first narrative throughout; 29 lessons carry 2 sub lessons, 3 are whole lessons (Gafat foundry, Boru Meda council, besieging Sahati). Full video script for the Adwa battle account where the placement names it. STUDENT BOOK ALONE: no teacher evidence claimed — every sources.md states the Grade 8 guide's Units 4–7 are not in hand. Date traps taught corrected and named in sources: Mekelle January 1896 (printed 1895), Maychew March 31, 1936 (printed May 31); the OAU correction (printed June 25 → May 25, 1963) recorded in the first lesson's sources as belonging to a later title, not taught here. Placement 109 (assessment stock) is a prove-it pool, built into its own lessons; the one unbuilt piece is the old-titles definitions task (Fitawrari, Ras, Dejazmach, Bitweded, Grazmach, Lij) — the placements use the titles but give no definitions, so per the guide they were not taught. Pictures 11600–11631 (32, drawn PIL, every one looked at via contact sheets — all clean, corrected dates verified on 11617 and 11625); GIFs 11632 (caravan Bonga to Gondar) and 11633 (two invasion arrows converging on Addis) frame-checked PASS. Build log: ethiopia-2026-11-11/build-logs/ss-K-modern-ethiopia.md.

ethiopia-2026-11-11/build-logs/wave4-hist-B-g10-1500-1900.md.

Tsion. All story-first with story-style animated-map video scripts;
guide-only content labelled (teacher guide — evidence) everywhere.
Pictures 22500–22524 (25 drawn PIL, every one read); GIFs 22530–22533
all frame-checked PASS. Honesty carried: the Unit 3 numbering
mismatch, the false "Zagwe replaced Aksum in 1270" line, "Chon"→Zhou,

named Gurage systems (absent; the book's Gurage content is inside

moving court, Napoleon). Build log:
ethiopia-2026-11-11/build-logs/wave4-hist-A-g09-ancient.md.

only (Köppen zones of Ethiopia; the geographer's questions) and

(student governs), Panthalassa and Sanetti misprints corrected,
Nile 55.5 vs 58.5 BCM named, landlocked 49 taught over the summary's
"over 30", "Easter Boserup" corrected. The brief-list topics the
G11 book does not teach (plate boundaries, folding, volcanism,
weathering as named lessons) were not built — no page, no lesson;
the G7–8 lessons on them stand untouched. The band's media was
first built at 21600–21730 and renumbered +5400 into 27000–27132
on the coordinator's order after another stream took the 21600s.
Build log:
ethiopia-2026-11-11/build-logs/wave4-geo-C-g11-physical.md.

## Ethiopia Economics — the four titles open (Wave 4, 2026-11-12)

Economics stocks inside Social Studies in four NEW titles, added
to the path in TITLES.md after Global Issues: **Microeconomics**,
**Market and Money**, **Macroeconomics**, **Ethiopia and
Development** (some lessons also land in Global Issues and
People and Resources, where the books put them). Bands register
here as they land; counts are per band from the build logs.

- **Band D — Grade 11 second part + guide** (57 created + 2
  extended, 708 new questions; pictures 24900–24958, GIFs
  24970–24972): consumption/saving/investment (incl. Iqub —
  saving together the Ethiopian way, and the accelerator), the
  trade and finance set, the development set (calculating an
  HDI, the capability approach, Ethiopia's development plans)
  and the Ethiopia sectors set; the balance of payments and the
  ADLI lesson extended. Its cut line: Units 4–7, placements
  48–106 (Band C takes Units 1–3). Errors taught corrected and
  named in sources: the 80 birr is autonomous, not "induced";
  the HDI goalposts follow the worked example (the only reading
  that reproduces the book's own 0.75); the guide key's "HDI"
  PQLI item corrected to the Physical Quality of Life Index.
- **Band F — Grade 12 teacher guide (evidence)** (14 lessons
  created, 168 questions; pictures 25500–25513): the guide is
  the **2011 GEQIP** edition, NOT the companion to the 2023
  student book — everything it stocks is labelled older
  evidence, never an answer key for the 2023 book. 26 placements
  folded into 14 standing lessons as labelled "older evidence"
  sections; 14 standalone guide-only lessons created in Ethiopia
  and Development (resource base, development plans, unimodal /
  bimodal strategies, package projects, the service sectors,
  structural adjustment 1993–1996, the 1990s scoreboard,
  investing after 1991), each carrying the 0-countries line.
  50/50 placements accounted. The guide's §6.5 review key
  (five birr results and a deficit figure) is unverifiable —
  no workings in the placements — so it is NOT taught as worked
  answers; "Gross Domestic Service" corrected to Gross Domestic
  Saving in sources.
- **Band B — Grade 10 student** (54 created + 2 extended, 672
  questions; pictures 24300–24355, GIFs 24400–24416): the
  Grade 10 book stocked across the four titles, stacking
  Grade 10 depth onto two Grade 9 lessons. The book's
  References list is truncated at A–K (Keefer & Knack) —
  carried as a record note in the road-map lesson's sources
  and in About, NOT a teaching gap.
- **Band C — Grade 10 guide + Grade 11 Units 1–3** (46 created
  + 12 extended, 633 new questions; pictures/GIFs in
  24600–24899): G11 placements 1–47 in full (cut line verified
  against Band D's: D starts at placement 48, Unit 4, printed
  p. 90 — the two halves meet exactly), plus the G10 guide's
  evidence stacked into 12 standing lessons, including a
  corrected surplus calculation and corrected Activity 4.1 key

- **Band E — Grade 12 student, PARTIAL** (33 lessons, 497
  questions; pictures 25200–25232 incl. 7 GIFs): the original
  builder's session ended mid-band. A completion pass verified
  all 33 standing lessons in full (shape, question counts —

**Correction (coverage audit + fix, 2026-11-12):** the
closure claim above did NOT survive the independent
placement-by-placement audit: only 264 of 416 placements
stood with their own book cited at their own pages — the
Grade 12 student book had been built only for Units 6, 8
and 9 (33 placements taught from other grades' books with
no Grade 12 citation, 19 absent), Grade 9 Units 4, 6 and 9
were standing on later books' tellings (41 mismatches),
the guides' pedagogy layer was largely unfiled, and the
Grade 12 unit review sets had been excluded rather than
folded. The fix build closed all of it: 74 citation
mismatches repaired with each book's own rows at its own
pages (plus the missing G9/G12-distinct content taught —
the Balkan Wars, Suez 1956 and the 1979 Iranian
revolution, the welfare-state/IMF strand, the
one-party/coup pattern, the Marxian foundations), 78
not-stocked items built or filed (7 new lessons — Persia,
North Africa's caliphates, Kanem-Bornu and the Hausa
states, the Trans-Saharan trade, Moggasa and Guddifacha,
egalitarian systems, the world after 1945 — new parts for
the Inca, indigenous religions, settlement patterns, Kafa
and Yem; the nine Grade 12 review sets folded into
prove-it questions; the guides' pedagogy and the books'
own shapes filed with substance in ABOUT.md). History now
stands verified at 416 of 416 placements. Build log:
ethiopia-2026-11-11/build-logs/fix-history.md.

invented. Build log:

## Geography expansion — Belarus — built 2026-11-14

Belarus — 24 placements stocked from 1 distinct book that
carries Geography (3 records / 2 distinct books in total):
*Геаграфія Беларусі. 10 клас (Geography of Belarus,
Grade 10)* (student book, Belarusian, COMPLETE;
Народная асвета (Narodnaya Asveta), Minsk, 2012;
ISBN 978-985-03-1787-2; authors M. M. Brylevsky,
G. S. Smolyakov; 304 PDF pp., printed book 303 pp.;
read via its recorded archive.org source — the GitHub
grab route was failing). Belarus's own way: the whole
course is one country studied whole — position and the
history of its own geographical research first (including
Belarusian place-names), then nature component by
component, then its five natural provinces, population,
the economy sector by sector, Belarus in the world, and
finally each of its six regions in turn; every paragraph
opens with a "recall" rubric and closes with questions
and tasks. The second book, *Чалавек. Грамадства.

standing lessons — **The Land and Air**: Maps through

## Geography expansion — France — built 2026-11-14

France — 24 placements stocked from 1 distinct book that
carries Geography (4 records / 2 distinct books in total):
*Géographie 2de GT, Manuel Libre de Géographie, Seconde*
(student book, French, COMPLETE; Les Manuels Libres,
Région académique Île-de-France, dir. Anne Parillaud;
year not printed; no ISBN printed, digital manual,
CC BY-NC-SA 4.0; no PDF pagination — digital HTML manual
read in full through its complete Sommaire, 4 themes).
France's own way: every theme is built the same way — a
world chapter taught through one deep case study (Los
Angeles megafires, the Amazon, Japan, the Mediterranean),
then the same theme re-taught on France itself, closing
with map-sketch (croquis) and mind-map assessment; debate
and role-play (nuclear debate, G7 simulation) are standard
geography methods. The second book, *Histoire 2de, Manuel
Libre d'Histoire, Seconde* (student book, French), is
RECORD-ONLY — pure History, the History half of the
Seconde Histoire-Géographie programme; no Geography
chapters; not placed, a later History wave owns it.
Stocked into standing lessons — **Earth and Space**:
Landforms, continents and oceans (Sommaire Thème 1 —
now taught in 3 countries, Ethiopia, Saudi Arabia and
France); **Resources and Conflict**: Water under pressure
(Thème 1 — now 3 countries, Ethiopia, Brazil and France)
and Fights over water, land, oil and diamonds (Thème 1,
the méga-bassines at Sainte-Soline — now 2 countries);
**Global Issues**: The sustainability challenge (Thème 1,
world energy consumption — now 2 countries);
**Population, Transport and Maps**: Ageing countries —
the support ratio (Thème 2, Japan case — now 2
countries), The demographic transition model (Thème 2 —
now 2 countries), Maps and globes — kinds of maps and
scale (Thème 2 bilan, croquis of France — now 2
countries), Kinds of migration (Thème 3, Mediterranean
case — now 5 countries, Ethiopia, Micronesia, Brazil,
Belarus and France) and Push and pull — why Africans move
(Thème 4, Southern Africa migrations — now 2 countries);
**People and Resources**: Growth is not development —
the HDI (Thème 2, with a G7 simulation — now taught in
3 countries, Ethiopia, Brazil and France; Argentina stays
teacher-guide evidence only) and Tourism — selling the
landscape (Thème 3, Mediterranean tourism basin — now
taught in 2 countries, Ethiopia and France; Morocco stays
teacher-guide evidence only) — and 13 new lessons built
— in **Global Issues**: Societies facing risks — Los
Angeles megafires (Thème 1), France's energy transition
— the nuclear debate (Thème 1), Risk management in
France — Dunkirk (Thème 1) and Southern Africa —
transition and development challenges (Thème 4); in
**Resources and Conflict**: The Amazon forest — value
and protect (Thème 1) and France — inequalities and

provincial products, and the protected-area system

thematic; Geography enters as a full cartography tema

## Geography expansion — Bangladesh — built 2026-11-14

Bangladesh — 61 placements stocked from 10 records / 5
distinct grade books (each in Bangla + English parallel
editions), all student books, National Curriculum and
Textbook Board (NCTB), Dhaka, National Curriculum 2022,
all COMPLETE, read via grab.py from the GitHub textbooks
repo (English read fully for Grades 6-7, Bangla read
fully for Grades 8 and 9-10, the other edition
spot-checked — same content, placed once for both
editions). Placed books: *History and Social Science,
Class Six* (Grade 6, 2022, 184 PDF pp.; 22 placements),
*History and Social Science, Class Seven* (Grade 7,
2022, 184 PDF pp.; 15 placements), *History and Social
Science, Class Eight* (Grade 8, 2023, 188 PDF pp.; 12
placements) and *History and Social Science, Class
Nine* (Grades 9-10, 2023, 192 PDF pp.; 12 placements) —
the shared Class 9-10 SSC book: the Grade 10 tree files
are byte-identical to the Grade 9 files (English
50,152,317 bytes, SHA
d2a8d461e799677e306cfe71a92fd0637327ea1c; Bangla
8,397,648 bytes, SHA
43cedf3b16cdd5946012cc8c53cd143a848d5570), so the one
read covers all four G9/G10 records, placed once under
its true identity, exactly as the placements state.
All four are COMBINED History and Social Science books:
Geography parts are placed; History/Civics parts are
named in each lesson's sources, not placed.
Bangladesh's own way (Grade 6): an inquiry-based course
built around a class teacher character and group
investigations; Geography is taught through the
landform of Bengal itself — rivers, silt and hills
explaining settlement, ancient states, ports and even
the 1971 war — plus hands-on map-making and a pupil-run
nature conservation club. (Grade 7): Geography thins
into economic history and a long Sustainable
Development inquiry — pupils build a delta in a sand
tray, trace the rivers that enter Bangladesh from other
countries, map the new maritime boundary and minerals,
and audit groundwater and fossil fuels. (Grade 8): one
long chapter, "Protect the Bond between Nature and
People" — modelling the world's biomes, comparing
world forest cover in 2000 and 2020, mapping
Bangladesh's forests and disaster-prone areas, and an
inquiry-reader on biomes, sea resources and the
Sundarbans. (Grades 9-10): the "Living Together Safely"
chapter is a genuine geography course inside the
combined book — Khulna land-use change 1988-vs-2017,
world population and density, remote sensing and GIS,
settlement and urbanisation, desertification, and
Bangladesh's squeeze of limited land, dense population
and climate risk. Stocked into standing lessons (49
placements) across First Steps, The Land and Air,
Population, Transport and Maps, People and Resources,
Culture and Work, Resources and Conflict and Global
Issues. 11 NEW lessons built (12 placements; River
Cities and Ports of Old Bengal carries two, Grades 6
and 7) — **The Land and Air**: River Cities and Ports
of Old Bengal (G6, printed pp. 60-62; G7, pp. 55-59),
River Valleys and Coasts — Cradles of Civilisation
(G6, pp. 68-73), Landforms of Bangladesh (G6, p. 121),
Mapping Mineral Resources of Bangladesh and the World
(G7, pp. 151-153), The World's Biomes (G8, pp. 128-131),
Forests of Bangladesh and the Sundarbans (G8,
pp. 150-154) and Land-Use Change — Maps Over Time
(Grades 9-10, pp. 125-129); **Resources and Conflict**:
A School Nature Conservation Club (G6, pp. 77-81);
**Global Issues**: Bangladesh's New Sea Boundary and
Blue Economy (G7, pp. 142-144), Natural Disasters of
Bangladesh and How to Prepare (G8, pp. 155-159) and
Bangladesh's Land, People and Climate Challenge
(Grades 9-10, pp. 155-156) — the new lessons taught in
1 country (Bangladesh), 8 questions each (River Cities
and Ports of Old Bengal, with three sub lessons, 12
questions).

Library debts carried exactly as the placements state:
the books.json records for all 4 titles carry tree
paths that return 404 — the files actually live in
GitHub releases mid-size-books-2 and mid-size-books-7,
so the in_pushed tree records are misleading; and the
books.json source_url for both student books is
truncated with "…" and cannot be used as recorded.
Indonesia's own way (Grade 11): the whole book is
organised around Indonesia itself — its strategic
position first, then its own biodiversity
(Wallace/Wallacea), its own environment-and-population
problems, and its own disaster map; every concept is
taught through Indonesian examples, maps and
infographics, with a chapter-end knowledge assessment
(Asesmen) per chapter. (Grade 12): applied human
geography built entirely on Indonesian cases —
regional development and spatial planning (tata ruang)
measured against a Happiness Index, Industry 4.0 /
Society 5.0, and Indonesia's own geopolitics and
ASEAN-era cooperation as pillars of national
resilience. Stocked into standing lessons (30
placements) across Earth and Space, The Land and Air,
Population, Transport and Maps, People and Resources,
Culture and Work, Resources and Conflict and Global
Issues. 30 NEW lessons built (39 placements) —
**First Steps**: Teaching Geography in the Merdeka
Curriculum — Fase F Outcomes (Grades 11-12 teacher
guides, printed pp. 2 and 1 — teacher-guide evidence
only, counted nowhere); **Earth and Space**:
Indonesia's Geographic Position at the World
Crossroads (G11, p. 7; G11 guide, p. 67); **The Land
and Air**: Indonesia's Geological Position — Effects
and Resource Management (G11, p. 21), World Biome
Systems and Their Distribution (G11, p. 73), Wallace's
Eight Biogeographic Regions (G11, p. 79; G11 guide,
p. 139) and Indonesia's Flora and Fauna Distribution
and Its Factors (G11, p. 84); **Population, Transport
and Maps**: Population Quality — Health, Education and
Human Resource Development (G11, p. 145), Indonesia's
Population Problems and Their Solutions (G11, p. 155),
Regional Development and Regionalisation in Indonesia
(G12, p. 6; G12 guide, p. 34), Indonesian Villages —
Development Stages and Problems (G12, p. 35), City
Types and Spatial Structure Theories (G12, p. 44),
Spatial Planning (Tata Ruang) and Its Dynamics (G12,
p. 63; G12 guide, p. 34) and Indonesia's Demography and
Urbanisation Towards 2045 (G12, p. 121); **People and
Resources**: Indonesia's Natural Resource Potential
and Distribution (G11, p. 28), The Happiness Index as a
Result of Regional Development (G12, p. 75), How
Regional Development and Spatial Planning Affect
People's Happiness (G12, p. 82), Development —
Meanings, Paradigms, Approaches and Indicators (G12,
p. 97), Population Welfare as the Outcome of
Development (G12, p. 140) and How Regional Development
and Industrial Revolution Change Welfare (G12,

17 distinct books — the combined Social Studies student
books and teacher guides for Grades 1–12 (Government of

History, Civics, values/life-skills and (Grades 1–5)
Creative Arts, with the Geography parts placed and the

Directive, 2082 BS, a Ministry of Communication and
Information Technology administrative directive, not a
textbook; it is carried as filed, and the real Grade 11
Social Studies and Life Skills Education (English
version) book is NOT in the library — a library debt.
Book 17 (np-g8) is the old kingdom-era standalone

Janak Shiksha Samagri Kendra), a genuine Nepal Grade 8
geography textbook placed as such, but NOT the
current-curriculum Grade 8 social studies book (record
mismatch carried in its book entries). Nepal's own way:
Geography begins as 'Our Earth' at home scale (the land
around home and school, sketch maps), grows through the
district and a first full geography of Nepal (three
ecological regions — Himalayan, Hilly, Terai — physical
features, climate, vegetation, soils,
rivers/glaciers/lakes), widens to neighbours and
continents, and closes federally and applied (the seven
provinces, inter-provincial relations, disasters, GIS,
urbanisation and migration); the old Grade 8 volume
teaches Nepal through its 14 zones (anchal). The
student books count toward acceptance; the teacher
guides (Books 7, 10, 11, 14) are cited as the evidence
they are, outside acceptance counts. Stocked into

## Mozambique — Geography expansion (merged 2026-11-15)
257 placement lines stocked from Mozambique's 24
records / 12 distinct books — the PESD (Programa do
Ensino Secundário à Distância) distance-learning
Geography modules, Modules 1–3 of the first cycle
(Grades 7–9, filed as Grade 7) and Modules 1–9 of the
second cycle (Grades 10–12, filed as Grade 10), all
student books in Portuguese (Ministério da Educação e
Desenvolvimento Humano / Ministério da Educação,
Instituto de Educação Aberta e à Distância — IEDA; 2023
and 2022 uploads; no ISBN printed), each record
appearing twice (TSV + books.json, same repo path) and
read once per distinct book via the record's IEDA
source URL after grab.py failed on both GitHub routes;
verified by pdfinfo size against the books.json record
and cover/title page for every book. The placements
file's per-book tallies sum to 257 and agree with the
line-by-line count — no discrepancy (three Verdict
lines also mention printed pages, carrying the books'
own contents/lesson-numbering errors for Modules PESD
II 3, 4 and 7; they are verdict notes, not placements).
All 12 books are COMPLETE and all are student books, so
every placement counts toward acceptance. Book verdict
quirks are carried exactly in each book entry: PESD I
Module 2's Lição 13 body heading repeats 'actividade
pecuária' wording (the book's own copy error); PESD II
Module 3's contents page omits Lição 2 'Rochas'
(present in the body); PESD II Module 4 mis-numbers two
lessons as 'Lição 6' and has no Lição 5 heading; PESD
II Module 7's contents page skips the Lição 3 number
(present in the body); the PESD II Modules 6 and 8
title pages misprint the subject word ('Gografia' /
'Geogafia') and Module 9's title page labels the
subject slot 'Disciplina'. Mozambique's own way:
distance-learning self-study modules — the first cycle
follows the four Earth spheres as its organising spine,
then teaches human/economic geography sector by sector
(world-then-Mozambique), then turns to Mozambique
itself as a complete regional study closing with
Southern Africa and SADC integration; the second cycle
opens with geographic thought and cosmography, then
runs full physical courses (bioclimatic environment,
geomorphology, pedogeography and hydrogeography) and
human courses (population, agriculture and livestock,
industry and commerce,
transport/communications/tourism, cities). Stocked into
standing lessons (184 placements across 141 lessons) —
4 placements remapped, original slug recorded in each
provenance row: the-land-and-air/the-water-cycle was
flagged NEW (2 placements) but the lesson already

not stand and goes to
culture-and-work/secondary-activities-what-manufacturing-is;
the-land-and-air/drawing-a-watershed-boundary does not
stand and goes to
the-land-and-air/drainage-patterns-what-rivers-draw. 60

## Iraq — Geography expansion (merged 2026-11-15)
312 placement lines stocked from Iraq's 17 records / 10
distinct books (each grade+subject appears in both a
TSV record and a books.json record — the 17 records
collapse to 10 distinct books, duplicates taught once,
every distinct book cited). All 10 books are Arabic
student books of the Republic of Iraq, Ministry of
Education, General Directorate of Curricula, 1445 AH /
2023 CE, COMPLETE (Book 4 is a History book whose
Geography setting sections only are placed), read from
the files recorded in the books.json records' own
Google Drive source files after the GitHub route failed
repeatedly with RemoteDisconnected — same books, covers
and contents verified; printed page = PDF page except
Book 10 (Earth Science), whose printed pagination runs
6 behind. The placements file's tallies sum to exactly
312, matching the line count — no tally discrepancy.
The file's split is 163 existing-flagged / 149
NEW-flagged; by lesson dir, 183 placements land in 114
standing lessons and 129 in the 92 new lessons: 3
NEW-flagged slugs already stand as lessons
(culture-and-work/tourism-geography,
global-issues/noise-pollution and
global-issues/states-location-shape-and-borders) and
are stocked there, not rebuilt; 38 placements are
remapped from 31 slugs that do not stand, or
NEW-flagged slugs a standing lesson already teaches
under another slug, to the closest standing lesson
covering the thing, with the original slug recorded in
every remapped provenance row (see iraq_common.REMAP);
and the one existing-flagged placement for Iraq's
governorates lands in the new governorates lesson at
its own slug, that slug being overwhelmingly
NEW-flagged (20 of its 21 placements). Iraq's own way:
geography enters inside Social Studies through the
child's own surroundings (G4), then Iraq itself
location-first and region by region (G5), then
governorate by governorate in one fixed pattern (G6);
the ancient civilisations are each opened through their
land (G7), the whole Arab world follows as one
geographical unit (G8), and G9 returns to Iraq
measured; the literary track then treats geography as a
discipline (G10 foundations and techniques, G11
physical geography, G12 human geography, every chapter
closing with an applied study of Iraq), with the
scientific track's Earth Science at G11. Stocked into
standing lessons (183 placements across 114 lessons).

## Nicaragua — Geography expansion (merged 2026-11-15)
326 placement lines stocked from Nicaragua's 33 records
/ 13 distinct books. All 13 books are Spanish student
books of the Ministerio de Educación (MINED),
Nicaragua, all COMPLETE; Book 1 was read in place at
phase3-staging and Books 2–13 via the GitHub Releases
API route (grab.py), byte-size verified. The placements
file's header total and per-book tallies sum to exactly
326, matching the placement-line count — no tally
discrepancy. The file's split is 279 existing-flagged /
47 NEW-flagged (21 distinct NEW slugs); by lesson dir,
281 placements land in 83 standing lessons and 45 in
the 20 new lessons: one NEW-flagged slug already stands
as a lesson
(population-transport-and-maps/political-division-of-the-americas,
built by the Honduras merge) and its 2 placements are
stocked there, not rebuilt; no placement is remapped —
every existing-flagged slug stands. Book 10's record
name 'Primera Parte' is a misnomer carried exactly: the
book is the COMPLETE Grade 8 distance-learning module,
the parallel edition of Book 9. The non-Geography parts
of the combined books (History, Civics, Economics) are
named in the placements headers only, not placed.
Nicaragua's own way: Geography begins hyper-locally —
the child's own school, municipality and landscape, the
country introduced through its two great lakes
(Cocibolca and Xolotlán) as the 'land of lakes and
volcanoes' — then widens grade by grade: G3–G4 a full
national Geography at comunidad/municipio/departamento
scales, G5 the Central American isthmus, G6 the world
continents in one repeated template, G7 national
Geography closing on natural-risk prevention, G8 the
American continent, G9 the continents one by one, and
G10 the national Geography deepened, closing on
vulnerability and SINAPRED; distance-learning
self-study modules parallel the classroom books at G7,
G8, G9 (Geography only) and G10, every topic anchored
in the learner's own comunidad. Stocked into standing
lessons (281 placements across 83 lessons). 20 NEW
lessons built (45 placements) — **First Steps**: My
School And Its Surroundings (printed p. 39); **The Land
and Air**: Central America Climate (printed p. 39),
Central America Geological Evolution And Nicaragua
Geomorphological Regions (printed p. 49; printed p. 45;
printed p. 47), Central America Position Advantages And
Disadvantages (printed p. 22), Central America Relief
And Volcanic Chain (printed p. 30), Climates Of Europe

## Bhutan — Geography expansion (merged 2026-11-15)
349 placement lines stocked from Bhutan's 14 records /
9 distinct books. All 9 books are English student books
of the Royal Government of Bhutan (Center for School
Curriculum Development / Department of School
Education, Ministry of Education and Skills
Development), all COMPLETE; G4–G5 were read via the
grab.py tree route and G6–G12 via the records' ministry
(rec.gov.bt) source URLs after the grab.py route hung
on G6 — every book verified by pdfinfo with byte size +
page count matching its books.json record exactly, plus
cover/title-page render. The placements file's header
total and per-book tallies sum to exactly 349, matching
the placement-line count — no tally discrepancy. The
file's split is 205 existing-flagged / 144 NEW-flagged
(121 distinct NEW slugs); by lesson dir, 207 placements
land in 126 standing lessons and 142 in the 119 new
lessons: two NEW-flagged slugs already stand as lessons
(earth-and-space/the-rock-cycle and
population-transport-and-maps/models-of-urban-structure,
1 placement each) and their placements are stocked
there, not rebuilt; no placement is remapped — every
existing-flagged slug stands. The non-Geography parts
of the combined Social Studies books IV–VI
(Religion/History, Civics, Health/Life Skills) are
named in the placements headers only, not placed.
Bhutan's own way: Social Studies IV–VI integrates
Geography with Civics, Culture, Economics, History and
Religion, taught from the child's home, village and
district outward with Bhutanese materials and local
forest and hazard examples; Grade 5 anchors world
geography in Bhutan's own map (location, three physical
zones, neighbours, major rivers, protected areas) and
Grade 6 adds Bhutan's climate zones, hydropower,
urbanisation and glacial lake outburst floods (GLOF);
from Class VII Geography is a standalone subject in
three strands (Time and Space; Physical Environment;
People and Environment), every world topic closed with
its Bhutan case; the Intermediate level (IX–X) treats
each world topic at full textbook depth with the Bhutan
case inside it, and the Advanced level (XI–XII) is full
academic geography — map projections, remote sensing
and GIS, climate classification — with Bhutan applied
chapters on agriculture, manufacturing and tourism, the
last taught on Bhutan's own sustainability principle
("high value, low volume"). Stocked into standing
lessons (207 placements across 126 lessons). 119 NEW
lessons built (142 placements) — **Earth and Space**:

contact sheets. Armenia's own way: Grade 7 opens school
geography with the geographic map itself (plan, scale,
azimuth, projections) before any Earth science and runs
world economy branch by branch with Armenia as the
worked example; Grade 8 is a pure regional-geography
year on the UN scheme (position, nature, population,
economy for every region and chosen state, with
Armenia's four neighbours given full country lessons);
Grade 9 is the home country whole — the Armenian
Highlands, then the Republic section by section,
finishing marz by marz through all 10 provinces; Grade
10 is methods-first (history and branches of the
science, GIS/GPS/aerospace methods, lithosphere,
climate, hydrology, the political map, population,
resources measured by endowment); Grade 11 is the world
economy in depth followed by its ecological bill; Grade
12 is thematic — distinctive region types, then
humanity's global problems, closing on sustainable
development with mountain-country development read
through Armenia's own case; the Social Science course
runs person-first (man and nature, society, mind,
morality, beauty, law, economy, politics) and turns
practical in the senior years (market economy and
personal finance as life skills, the state's role
measured by GDP, growth, inflation and unemployment,
and a closing globalisation theme that includes global
problems' impact on Armenia itself). Stocked into
standing lessons (186 placements across 126 lessons).

taught as one integrated Man-Earth science — Grade 5
opens with the planet as a whole (globe and map skills
first, then the Universe and the Earth's four spheres);
Grade 6 is the home country whole, then region by
region, then the learner's own locality; Grade 7 is
physical geography sphere by sphere, every sphere
closing on rational use and sustainability; Grade 8 is
world economic and social geography (political map,
population, resources, production), every unit closing
with a practical tied to the learner's own
municipality, with Cuba as the comparison case; Grade 9
systematises the home country (archipelago, nature,
economic panorama, social development, environment,
regions, provinces and municipalities); Grade 11 is the
regional capstone — continents studied with one fixed
plan tipo (situation, physical character,
socio-economic character, regions, political map,
selected country studies); the workbooks are pure map
practice, nearly every activity done on an outline map.
Stocked into standing lessons (240 placements across 96

named there as teacher-guide evidence only, count
unchanged — and 7 new lessons that stand on a teacher
guide only (acceptance count 0 countries). Books 5 and
6 (S3 Geography student's book and teacher's guide,
Grade 11) are NOT in GitHub (books.json in_pushed:
false); they were read from the publisher source files
at the records' own source_urls (byte sizes matching
the records exactly) and staged at phase3-staging under
the 2026-11-13 rule — carried exactly in their book
entries. Book 24's PDF binds the same 72-page guide
twice; its content is counted once. South Sudan's own
way: Geography is built around South Sudan itself — its
own physical features, resources, floods/drought
experience and the Nile — in the secondary books, and
in the primary Social Studies books the land is taught
from the child's own payam outward (Mount Kinyeti, the
River Nile and the Sudd swamps as the named examples),
with paper-folding and paper-tearing practical
experiments, hand-made weather instruments and a school
weather station, and map work on the child's own
classroom, area and country. Stocked into standing
lessons (291 placements across 127 lessons). 131 NEW
lessons built (231 placements) — **First Steps**:

## Greece — Geography expansion (merged 2026-11-15)
599 placement lines stocked from Greece's 34 records /
17 distinct books (all Greek, Ministry of Education /
ITYE Diophantos). The placements file's per-book
tallies sum to exactly 599, matching the placement-line
count book by book, and its existing/NEW split
(134/465) agrees with the line flags (134
existing-flagged / 465 NEW-flagged, 179 distinct NEW
slugs) — no discrepancy. By lesson dir, 138 placements
land in 41 standing lessons and 461 in the 177 new
lessons. Two NEW-flagged slugs already stand as lessons
(global-issues/the-shrinking-aral-sea, 2 placements;
the-land-and-air/antarctica-the-frozen-continent, 2
placements — 4 placements in all) and their placements
are stocked there, not rebuilt. Two placements are
remapped from 2 existing-flagged slugs that do not
stand to the closest standing lesson covering the thing
(original slug recorded in each provenance row):
people-and-resources/economic-activities-the-three-levels
->
culture-and-work/economic-activities-the-three-levels
(1); the-land-and-air/materials-of-the-crust ->
earth-and-space/materials-of-the-crust (1). Books 1, 4,
10 and 14 are teacher guides (194 placements), cited as
teacher-guide evidence only, outside acceptance counts;
the workbooks (Books 3, 5, 9 and 12) carry STUDENT on
their Grade lines and count as student books. Every
standing destination takes at least one student-book
placement, so Greece counts in all 41 standing lessons;
5 new lessons stand on a teacher guide only (acceptance
count 0 countries). Books 6 and 16 are parallel revised
editions with 0 placements counted — their placements
are filed once under Books 2 and 11, both editions
named in those book entries. Books 7 and 8 (Social &
Political Education, Grades 5 and 6) are RECORD ONLY —
pure Civics, no Geography parts to place; a later
Civics wave owns them. Greece's own way: the primary
course studies one country whole — Grade 5 is 'Learning
Greece' (maps first, then Greece's natural environment,
then its human environment, closing outward to the EU,
Cyprus and the Greek diaspora) and Grade 6 turns
outward to the whole Earth ('Learning about the Earth':
Earth in space, the natural environment, how humans
live in deserts, polar lands, rainforests and temperate
zones, then a continent-by-continent tour, Europe first
and in depth); the Gymnasium course (Grades 7–8) is
organised by Earth's spheres in Grade 7 and gives the
whole of Grade 8 to one continent — Europe — with
Greece re-entered inside every theme; the EPAL Grade 12
book reads the world as tourism destinations, Greece
first and region by region. Stocked into standing
lessons (138 placements across 41 lessons). 177 NEW
lessons built (461 placements) — **The Land and Air**:

## Kazakhstan — Geography expansion (merged 2026-11-15)
740 placement lines stocked from Kazakhstan's 13 kz-b
books (Grades 7–9, all student books, all COMPLETE;
okulyk.kz textbooks from NIS, Atamura, Mektep and
Almatykitap). The placements file holds two reader
passes: kz-b filed Books 1–13 (740 placements) and kz-a
filed two extra sections — Book 1 (kz-a), Karatabanov
G7 Part 1 in Kazakh (tally 25), and Book 2 (kz-a),
Egorina G7 2025 in Russian (tally 65) — which duplicate
kz-b's Book 4 (Part 1 portion) and Book 2 respectively,
parallel editions of the same books placed once by
kz-b. The kz-a duplicates (90 tally placements) were
DROPPED, not stocked. The kz-b per-book tallies sum to
exactly 740, matching the kz-b placement-line count
book by book, and its existing/NEW split (347/393)
agrees with the line flags (347 existing-flagged / 393
NEW-flagged, 77 distinct NEW slugs) — no discrepancy in
kz-b. (kz-a's two sections carry 88 slug placement
lines against their 90 tally; the 2-line difference
sits entirely in the dropped kz-a sections.) By lesson
dir, 360 placements land in 70 standing lessons and 380
in the 75 new lessons. Two NEW-flagged slugs already
stand as lessons
(culture-and-work/international-division-of-labour, 7
placements;
the-land-and-air/ocean-water-temperature-and-salinity,
6 placements — 13 placements in all) and their
placements are stocked there, not rebuilt. No
existing-flagged slug is missing, so there are no
remappings. All 13 books are student books, so
Kazakhstan counts in every lesson it lands in and no
lesson stands on teacher-guide evidence. Parallel
Russian/Kazakh editions of one textbook are placed
once, every edition named in the book entry.
Kazakhstan's own way: three parallel Grade 7–9 course
families run side by side — the NIS inquiry books (goal
box and 'Your turn!' task block in every lesson,
trilingual glossary), the question-led Almatykitap
books (every paragraph titled as a question, 'My
geographic achievements' self-checks), and the
paragraph-numbered Atamura and Mektep courses with
coded learning goals — and the whole Grade 9 year in
every family is Kazakhstan itself: its relief, climate,
waters, natural zones, population and economy. Stocked
into standing lessons (360 placements across 70
lessons). 75 NEW lessons built (380 placements) —

## History expansion — Canada — built 2026-11-15

3 new lessons, 24 questions, in **The Modern World**:
Inuit whaling — the traditional hunt
(modern-world/canada-inuit-whaling-traditional),
Whalers from across the sea
(modern-world/canada-whalers-from-across-the-sea) and
Whaling today — protecting the whales
(modern-world/canada-whaling-today-and-protection)
— 4 placements stocked from Canada's *ᓴᖅᐱᓕᒐᓱᖕᓂᐅᕙᓚᐅᖅᑐᖅ
ᒥᒃᓵᓄᑦ (History of Whaling) — Uqalimaariuqsaniq Level 13*,
grade-01 record / Level 13 leveled reader, student book
in Inuktitut (Uqalimaariuqsaniq Inuktitut reading
program, Nunavut): why the whale was hunted and what it
gave — bones for tools and sleds, oil for lamps, maktaq
for food (printed pp. 8–11); whaling the old way —
Elders' knowledge, open boats, hand harpoons, the whole
camp sharing (printed p. 12); foreign commercial whalers
hunting in large numbers for oil and baleen, changing
Inuit life through trade and depletion (printed p. 22);
whales protected and counted today, with Inuit whaling
continuing as a small, community-run hunt under shared
rules (printed pp. 28–32). History lessons: 249 → 252.

## History expansion — Argentina — built 2026-11-15

(Ministerio de Educación, Ciencia y Tecnología de la
Nación, Buenos Aires, 2007, 1st ed., ISBN
978-950-00-0629-3): thinking about the State — one
national state built with army, laws, schools and
railways (printed pp. 59–63); stories and images of a
country changing fast — Buenos Aires and the pampas
transformed in one generation (printed p. 64); the
changes in Western Europe and the pull of land and
work (printed pp. 69–74); the State's role and the
1876 Immigration and Colonisation Law — by 1914
nearly a third foreign-born, mostly Italy and Spain
(printed pp. 74–80); the State and foreign investment
— British and other foreign capital in the railways
and meat-packing plants (printed p. 80); and the
conquest of indigenous territories and "Progress"
does not reach everyone — the army against the
indigenous peoples of the south and the Chaco, and
immigrant families crowded into conventillo
tenements (printed pp. 84–100). The book's Geography
(Axis 1, printed pp. 32–55) and Civics (Axis 3,
printed pp. 104–129) parts are named in the lessons'
sources, not placed here. All 6 placements stand on
the teacher guide as evidence only — every lesson's
acceptance count is 0 countries, counted nowhere.

## History expansion — Zambia — built 2026-11-15

4 new lessons, 32 questions, in **Africa after 1500**:
Zambia — primary resistance in Northern Rhodesia
(africa-after-1500/zambia-primary-resistance-in-northern-rhodesia),
Zambia — secondary resistance and the road to
independence
(africa-after-1500/zambia-secondary-resistance-and-the-road-to-independence),
Zambia — liberation struggle sites
(africa-after-1500/zambia-liberation-struggle-sites)
and Zambia — nationalists of the independence struggle
(africa-after-1500/zambia-nationalists-of-the-independence-struggle)
— 7 placements stocked from Zambia's *History Teaching
Module — Form 2 Term 3*, Grade 8 (Form 2), teacher
guide in English (Republic of Zambia, Ministry of
Education, Curriculum Development Centre, 2026, module
for the 2024 History Syllabus / Competence Based
Curriculum, no ISBN printed): primary resistance —
the Lunda tax refusal at Mwinilunga 1907, the Gwembe
Tonga 1909, Namwanga and Bisa refusing forced labour,
the Ngoni under Mpezeni I 1897 and Mwata Kazembe,
defending land, chiefs, chitemene farming and
independent churches (printed pp. 2–6); secondary
resistance — welfare associations, the Watchtower
movement banned 1935, Copperbelt strikes 1935 and
1940, the Congress of 1948 becoming the ANC in 1951,
the London delegation, the 1953 Federation fight,
boycotts, pass-burning and UNIP's Cha Cha Cha campaign
of 1961, to independence in 1964 (printed pp. 7–10);
the liberation places — Mulungushi Rock of Authority
and the Mukuyu Tree at Kabwe, Chilenje House No. 394,
Freedom House, Matero House No. 3144B, Bwacha House
No. E1376, the Freedom Statue and Isa ya Mwaamba at
Choma (printed pp. 11–13); the leading nationalists
(printed pp. 15–19) and the other freedom fighters,
including Mutembo of the Freedom Statue and the
Mposa Mabwe stone throwers of Kitwe (printed
pp. 20–22). Two further placements stocked into
existing lessons: honouring the nationalists — role
play and the one-minute Salute Wall — into the First
Steps heritage lesson (printed pp. 23–25), and the
model lesson plan on the development of slavery and
the slave trade into the Atlantic slave trade lesson
(printed pp. 27–31). Zambia's other held book, the
Grade 4 Social Studies Term 3 module, is RECORD-ONLY
for History (Environment, Entrepreneurship and Money
only) and is named in the new lessons' sources, not
placed. All 7 placements stand on the teacher guide
as evidence only — every new lesson's acceptance
count is 0 countries, counted nowhere, and the two
existing lessons' acceptance counts do not change.
History lessons: 257 → 261.

## History expansion — Nauru — built 2026-11-15

1 new lesson, 8 questions, in **The Ancient World**:
Pacific voyaging and island exchange
(ancient-world/pacific-voyaging-and-island-exchange)
— 8 placements stocked from Nauru's three regional
USP/UNESCO ESD books: *People and the Sea in the
Pacific — Book 1*, Years 5–6 student reader (Institute
of Education, University of the South Pacific (Tonga
Campus) with UNESCO, sponsored by the Japanese
Funds-in-Trust, 2020, no ISBN printed): "The Ocean in
our blood" — Fiji, Samoa, Tonga, Niue, Rotuma, Tokelau,
Tuvalu, Futuna and Uvea once part of one large exchange
community, peoples regularly voyaging between islands
circulating wealth, skills and goods (printed p. 3),
into the new lesson; sharks, turtles and whales in
Pacific culture (printed pp. 7–12) and traditional
uses of the reef and ocean, traditional fishing in
Tuvalu and Niue (printed pp. 13–17), into the existing
First Steps heritage lesson. *People and the Natural
Environment in the Pacific — Book 3*, Years 5–6 student
reader (same publisher, 2020): knowledge passed down
through generations (printed pp. 6–7), taro in Tonga,
Niue and Tuvalu (printed pp. 8–11) and the amazing
coconut tree (printed pp. 12–15), all into the heritage
lesson. *Teacher Guide for the ESD Readers — Years
5–8*, teacher guide (same publisher, 2020), cited as
evidence only: pupils sent to talk to an elder about
sacred and traditional practices and traditional
fishing (printed p. 20), into the existing First Steps
find-history-in-your-own-locality lesson — Nauru is
not added to that lesson's count, as the guide is
evidence only; and the coconut-palm inheritance lesson
(printed p. 28), into the heritage lesson as evidence.
Nauru was not counted in the heritage lesson before,
so its acceptance count rises 16 → 17 countries.
Library debt, as filed: Nauru's shelf holds no Nauru
national history book at all — only these regional
readers, byte-identical to the files also shelved
under Niue (MD5-verified). History lessons: 261 → 262.

existing lesson's acceptance count does not change.

## History expansion — Tuvalu — built 2026-11-15

1 new lesson, 8 questions, in **The Modern World**:
European whaling in the Pacific
(modern-world/european-whaling-in-the-pacific)
— 10 placements stocked from Tuvalu's three regional
USP/UNESCO ESD books: *People and the Sea in the
Pacific — Book 1*, Years 5–6 student reader (Institute
of Education, University of the South Pacific (Tonga
Campus) with UNESCO, sponsored by the Japanese
Funds-in-Trust, 2020, no ISBN printed): "The Ocean in
our blood" and the old exchange community — Fiji,
Samoa, Tonga, Niue, Rotuma, Tokelau, Tuvalu, Futuna
and Uvea once one large exchange community, peoples
voyaging between islands circulating wealth, skills
and goods (printed pp. 1–2), stocked into the existing
Ancient World lesson Pacific voyaging and island
exchange — a NEW LESSON NEEDED placement in the
Tuvalu file, stocked into the lesson built for the
same topic in the Nauru merge instead of building a
duplicate; sharks, turtles and whales in Pacific
culture, with the sacred Tongan shark-noosing ritual
and turtle taboos (printed pp. 6–10), and traditional
uses of the reef and ocean with Tuvalu's own
practices on Nukufetau (printed pp. 12–15), into the
existing First Steps heritage lesson; and whales
after the Europeans came — Pacific people used only
stranded whales, teeth for chiefs' necklaces, until
North American whaling fleets reached Tonga in the
1890s and hunted mothers and calves, driving the
whales near extinction in one century before legal
protection let them slowly recover (printed p. 10),
into the new lesson. *People and the Natural
Environment in the Pacific — Book 3*, Years 5–6
student reader (same publisher, 2020): knowledge
passed down through generations (printed p. 6),
Tuvalu's pulaka pits (printed pp. 7–9) and the
amazing coconut tree, with Tuvalu's red toddy
(printed pp. 11–13), all into the heritage lesson.
*Teacher Guide for the ESD Readers — Years 5–8*,
teacher guide (same publisher, 2020), cited as
evidence only: pupils sent to talk to an elder, list
traditional fishing practices and find a legend of
the turtle, whale or shark (printed pp. 16–20), into
the existing First Steps
find-history-in-your-own-locality lesson — Tuvalu is
not added to that lesson's count, as the guide is
evidence only; the Book 3 inheritance notes (printed
pp. 24–28) and the Tuvalu-specific notes — the closed
Namo lagoon, storytelling and fatele song, and
Culture and Heritage in the Tuvalu National
Curriculum Framework (printed pp. 38–41) — into the
heritage lesson as evidence. Tuvalu was not counted
in the heritage lesson before, so its acceptance
count rises 18 → 19 countries, and in the Pacific
voyaging lesson 2 → 3 countries. Library debt, as
filed: Tuvalu's shelf holds no Tuvalu national
history book at all — only these regional readers,
the same set also shelved under Nauru and Niue.
History lessons: 270 → 271.

## History expansion — Saudi Arabia — built 2026-11-15

5 new lessons, 40 questions: in **First Steps**:
Cause and effect in history
(first-steps/cause-and-effect-in-history); in **The
Ancient World**: The Arabian Peninsula — cradle of
civilisations
(ancient-world/arabian-peninsula-cradle-of-civilisations)
and The Arabian Peninsula before Islam
(ancient-world/arabian-peninsula-before-islam); in
**The Modern World**: Saudi Arabia — Founding Day
and National Day
(modern-world/saudi-arabia-founding-day-and-national-day)
and Saudi Arabia — unification under Abdulaziz:
the brave courier
(modern-world/saudi-arabia-unification-under-abdulaziz)
— 11 placements stocked from Saudi Arabia's one
distinct book: *الدراسات الاجتماعية — الصف الرابع
الابتدائي — غير مجزأ (Social Studies, Grade 4
Primary, unabridged full-year edition)*, Grade 4
student book (المركز الوطني للمناهج (National
Centre for Curricula), Riyadh, 1446هـ, ISBN
978-603-8576-98-8; the record title gives the year
as 1448هـ, the held copy is the 1446 edition):
the concept of history — history as the study of
past events and interpreting them, and the
historian's questions (printed pp. 40–42), into
the existing First Steps What history is lesson;
sources — primary sources against secondary
sources, and the duty toward false news (printed
pp. 43–46), into From evidence to a written
history; cause and effect — every event has causes
and results, practised on examples the children
choose and sort (printed pp. 47–50), into the new
lesson; chronological order — the timeline built
from Saudi history itself: the unification
(1351هـ), the death of King Abdulaziz (1373هـ),
King Salman's accession (1436هـ) (printed
pp. 51–54), into Dating the past; "The Brave Man"
story activity — Abdulaziz, having secured the
roads and unified the country, sends a secret
letter by a brave courier along a watched road
(printed pp. 55–57), into the new Modern World
lesson; national symbols — Founding Day 1727,
National Day 1932 and Flag Day (printed
pp. 24–28), into the new lesson; the Arabian
Peninsula as the Arabs' homeland and a cradle of
civilisations — 'Ad, Thamud, Madyan, al-Maqar,
Dadan, the Nabataeans and Kinda, its history in
rock inscriptions (printed pp. 162–165), into the
new lesson; the peninsula before Islam — Tasm and
Jadis, a tribal land with no single state, values
of courage, generosity and protecting the
neighbour, Khadija's caravans and Makkah's caravan
wealth (printed pp. 166–169), into the new lesson;
Makkah and the Kaaba, Madinah and Masjid
al-Qiblatain (printed pp. 170–174), into the
existing Ancient World lesson The rise and
expansion of Islam; the Kingdom's antiquities —
Rajajil, al-Hijr, al-Ukhdud, Juwatha Mosque and
the National Museum (printed pp. 175–177), into
the existing First Steps heritage lesson; and the
prophets — Adam and Noah, Ibrahim and Hajar, Musa
at Madyan near Tabuk, and Isa (printed
pp. 184–202), into the existing Ancient World
lesson Faiths that shaped states. Saudi Arabia was
not counted in any of these lessons before, so
their acceptance counts rise: What history is
1 → 2, From evidence to a written history 1 → 2,
Dating the past 2 → 3, heritage 19 → 20, The rise
and expansion of Islam 1 → 2, Faiths that shaped
states 1 → 2. History lessons: 271 → 276.

The Valladolid debate
(modern-world/the-valladolid-debate), The making of

The Edict of Nantes and its revocation
(modern-world/the-edict-of-nantes-and-its-revocation),
Versailles and absolute monarchy
(modern-world/versailles-and-absolute-monarchy),
England's constitutional revolutions
(modern-world/englands-constitutional-revolutions)
and France before the Revolution — tensions of the
old regime
(modern-world/france-before-the-revolution-tensions-of-the-old-regime)
— 28 placements stocked from France's 1 distinct
book read here: the Grade 10 (Seconde) student book
*Histoire 2de — Manuel Libre d'Histoire, Seconde*
(Les Manuels Libres, Région académique
Île-de-France; year not printed; licence CC BY-NC-SA
4.0; no ISBN printed): Thème 1, La Méditerranée
antique (Périclès; L'empreinte grecque; L'empreinte
romaine; Constantin), into the existing Athens and
democracy, Greece-Crete, Rome and Rome divides
lessons; Thème 1, La Méditerranée médiévale (Les
formes de pouvoir; Des contacts diversifiés; Venise;
Bernard de Clairvaux), into the existing Byzantium
and Justinian and Trade routes lessons and the new
Venice and Crusades lessons; Thème 2, L'ouverture
atlantique (Autour de la notion de découverte;
Autour de la notion d'empire; L'or et l'argent des
Amériques; La controverse de Valladolid), into the
existing Exploration — Columbus and Aztecs lessons
and the new Atlantic economy and Valladolid lessons;
Thème 2, Renaissance et réformes religieuses
(Érasme; la naissance de l'artiste; la chapelle
Sixtine; Luther; les causes des réformes), into the
existing Renaissance great figures and Reformation —
Luther lessons; Thème 3, L'affirmation de l'État
(Ordonnance de Villers-Cotterêts; Édit de Nantes et
révocation; La politique de Colbert; Versailles) and
Le monde britannique (Habeas Corpus et Bill of
Rights; Du modèle anglais au modèle américain;
Voltaire et l'Angleterre), into the existing American
independence lesson and the new Modern state in
France, Edict of Nantes, Versailles and England's
constitutional revolutions lessons; Thème 4,
Lumières et développement des sciences (L'esprit
scientifique; Galilée; Émilie du Châtelet; La machine
à vapeur), into the existing Scientific revolution
and Enlightenment and Industrial Revolution —
textiles, steam and rail lessons; and Thème 4,
Tensions, mutations et crispations (La révolte des
Va-nu-pieds; riches et pauvres à Paris; Les ports
français; Un salon au XVIIIe siècle), into the new
Atlantic economy and France before the Revolution
lessons. The manual is an HTML manual with no
printed pagination, so placements cite theme/chapter
instead of printed pages, exactly as filed. All 28
placements stand on the student book, counted in
acceptance (the nine new lessons; and the thirteen
existing lessons stocked, whose counts rise with
France added, as listed in TITLES.md). The other
France record, *Géographie 2de GT — Manuel Libre de
Géographie, Seconde*, is RECORD ONLY — pure
Geography, not placed, with 0 placements. History
lessons: 351 → 360.

Liberation movements
(africa-after-1500/liberation-movements),
Neocolonialism (africa-after-1500/neocolonialism),
The African Renaissance
(africa-after-1500/the-african-renaissance), Gender
and Africa's future
(africa-after-1500/gender-and-africas-future) and
African diasporas, ancient and modern
(africa-after-1500/african-diasporas-ancient-and-modern)
— 31 placements stocked from Djibouti's 1 distinct
History book read here, a teacher guide: the
*Prototype du guide pédagogique — Histoire générale
de l'Afrique, tranche d'âge 10-12 ans* (UNESCO HGA
pedagogical guide prototype; year and ISBN not
printed): UA1 (history and time, sources and methods,
first fossils, first tools), into the existing First
Steps What history is and From evidence to a written
history lessons and the existing Africa, cradle of
humankind and From stone tools to farming lessons;
UA2 (habitat, family, food, dress and art), into the
new Daily life in ancient Africa lesson, and UA2–UA3
(Sahara and rivers; trade circuits), into the existing
How Africa's regions traded with each other lesson;
UA3 (kingdoms and empires; court learning and art),
into the new Kingdoms, empires and other African
states lesson; UA4 (internal slavery, the trans-Saharan
and Indian Ocean trades, the Atlantic trade), into the
existing Slavery before the Atlantic trade, The Indian
Ocean and trans-Saharan slave trades and The Atlantic
slave trade lessons, and UA4 (resistance; slavery as a
crime against humanity), into the new Resistance to
slavery lesson; UA5 (great formations 1500–1900 and
their endogenous causes), into the new Internal
transformations lesson; UA6 (European presence, Berlin,
colonial systems), into the existing Explorers and
missionaries, Berlin Conference and Direct and indirect
rule lessons, and UA6 (resistance; liberation movements),
into the new African resistance and Liberation movements
lessons; UA7 (liberation process; Pan-Africanism, OAU,
African Union and the obstacles to unity), into the
existing Decolonisation and African unity lessons, and
UA7 (neocolonialism), into the new Neocolonialism
lesson; UA8 (new challenges), into the existing After
the flag lesson, and UA8 (African Renaissance; gender
and unity), into the new African Renaissance and Gender
lessons; and UA9 (diasporas, brain drain and diaspora
contributions), into the new African diasporas lesson.
The book is a teacher guide, so all 31 placements stand
as teacher-guide evidence only, outside acceptance
counts: the ten new lessons stand at 0 countries counted,
and the fourteen existing lessons stocked keep their
countries and counts unchanged, with Djibouti named in
their sources as evidence only. Library debt, carried
as filed: the book is not Djibouti-specific — it is the
UNESCO General History of Africa pedagogical-guide
prototype for ages 10–12, filed in the library under
Djibouti; Djibouti's own primary history/social-studies
book is still missing. History lessons: 370 → 380.

independence, p. 57). The Geography and Citizenship
lessons of each combined guide are named in the
lessons' sources, not placed. 33 placements were
stocked into 23 existing lessons (the placement for
African resistance to colonisation, marked NEW
LESSON NEEDED in the Gabon file, was stocked into
the existing lesson of that name built for Djibouti
instead of building a duplicate; its countries line
now names Djibouti, Gabon as teacher-guide evidence
only). All 52 placements stand on teacher guides
only, as evidence only — the new lessons' acceptance
count is 0 countries, and every existing lesson's
acceptance count is unchanged, Gabon not added to
its countries line. History lessons: 522 → 530.

Grade 8 CONED anthology (cr-coned-8, 96 PDF pp.) is

Geography (dynamic earth, climate change, adaptation
proposals), named in the placements file, not
placed; the History strand pauses for that year in
the CONED sequence. The books' non-History parts
stay named, not placed: the Grade 7 ecological
footprint and transformed geographic space units,
the Grade 10 population dynamics unit, and the
Grade 11 globalisation units of both books, plus
the Porras exam-practice unit. Costa Rica's own
way is stocked inside the lessons: the CONED
anthologies fuse History with Geography in one
Estudios Sociales course around a guiding question
per theme, land every world process in Costa Rica,
and turn fully homeward in Grade 11, where the
national 20th century — agro-export crisis,
democratic and social struggles, the 1948 civil
war and social reform, the welfare state and the
post-1980 neoliberal turn — is the history.

8 placements were stocked into 4 existing lessons
(What history is — two placements, from Classes IV
and VIII; Heritage — what it is and why it matters
— four placements, from Classes VI, VII, IX and X;
Who studies prehistory, and World religions — the
five main faiths). All seven books are student
books, so Bhutan counts normally everywhere it is
newly stocked: What history is rises 9 → 10, Who
studies prehistory 5 → 6, and World religions — the
five main faiths 1 → 2. Heritage — what it is and
why it matters already named and counted Bhutan
from an earlier stocking, so its acceptance count
stays 24; the four new placements add Bhutanese
depth, not a new country. Bhutan's own way is
stocked inside the lessons: History enters inside
integrated Social Studies as the story of the
child's own home, country and faith, becomes a
subject of its own from Class VII as a single
national story in chronological order — prehistory
from stones and spoken tales, heritage, saint and
hero biographies, the foreign encounter — is
re-told at Class VIII with open source-criticism,
turns at Class IX to kings as reformers judged by
what they dismantled or built, and closes at
Class X with law codes, Gross National Happiness,
a king who gives away his own power, and the
constitution signed in 2008. The books'
non-History parts (Geography, Civics, Economics
and environment chapters named in the placements
file) stay named in the stocked sources, not
placed.
History lessons: 594 → 635.

stocked: every stocked lesson's countries line names
Malaysia and its acceptance count rises by one (What
history is 10 → 11, From evidence to a written
history 8 → 9, Dating the past 5 → 6, Find history in
your own locality 6 → 7, From stone tools to farming
6 → 7, Mesopotamia 5 → 6, Ancient Egypt 7 → 8, India
3 → 4, China 2 → 3, Greece-Crete 6 → 7, Athens and
democracy 4 → 5, Rome 7 → 8, China's dynastic cycle
2 → 3, The rise and expansion of Islam 5 → 6, The
Arabian Peninsula before Islam 2 → 3, Nationalism
4 → 5, The Second World War — causes 4 → 5, The
Second World War — the Pacific and the results
5 → 6, What colonial rule did to Africa 2 → 3).
Two placements whose named slugs do not exist were
stocked into the closest existing lessons with the
original slug recorded in the provenance rows:
ancient-world/arabia-before-islam-trade-and-tribes
(Arab society before Islam, Tingkatan 1 Bab 8.1)
into The Arabian Peninsula before Islam, and
modern-world/colonialism-and-its-end (what Western
administration changed, Tingkatan 3 Bab 6) into What
colonial rule did to Africa — political, economic,
social, whose economic and social registers cover
the same thing. Malaysia's own way is stocked
inside the lessons: history begins at home in
Tahun 4 — biodata, family tree, school, hometown —
then widens to the Ice Age, the cave sites and the
Melaka heroes; Tingkatan 1 opens as "Our History
and the World's", method first with Malaysia's own
prehistory inside the world story; Tingkatan 2 is a
whole year of national heritage (Warisan Negara),
sultanate by sultanate down to Adat Perpatih and
Borneo's river governments; Tingkatan 3 teaches the
Western arrival state by state and names every
resister; Tingkatan 4 is the nation-building year
from Melaka as the first nation-state to Merdeka in
the rain at Stadium Merdeka; Tingkatan 5 is
Malaysia itself — the 1963 federation, Konfrontasi,
Singapore's exit, 13 May 1969, the Rukun Negara and
the New Economic Policy. The Tingkatan 5 book's
non-History parts (Bab 1 Sovereignty concepts,
Bab 3 Constitutional Monarchy and Parliamentary
Democracy, Bab 4 The Federal System, and Bab 9–10
Foreign Policy and Malaysia on the World Stage —
Civics/current affairs) stay named in the stocked
sources, not placed, except the Bab 9.8 OIC
placement, which is placed.
History lessons: 635 → 656.

Grade 9 and Grade 10 (Grade 8 interior) and Tercero de
Bachillerato (Grade 11 interior); Ecuador's real books
for those grades are not in hand — a library debt named
in the placements file. Ecuador's own way is stocked
inside the lessons: History enters at age 7 only as the
child's own past (Grade 2), Grade 5 tells one continuous
national story in period blocks (aboriginal roots,
Conquest, Gran Colombia, República I, II and III), the
Superior book (Grade 8) turns thematic and work-centred,
the Bachillerato Historia course bends the whole human
story home to Ecuadorian Indigenous women, the Inca
legacy and dollarised 21st-century Ecuador, the older
Grade 3 SM edition builds identity from the parish
outward, and the Acápite booklets pair the course with
Ecuadorian legends told as plot summaries only, never
transcribed. The books' non-History parts (Geography,
Civics, Economics and language/literature readings named
in the placements file) stay named in the stocked
sources, not placed.

Revised 1997, printed 2003) and the BGCSE History

placements file, not placed. The Grade 5 textbook's
verdict is PARTIAL as filed: one stray Grade 4
"Bodies of Water" page (PDF p. 16) carrying only a
references line; all Grade 5 topics are otherwise
complete. Bahamas's own way is stocked inside the
lessons: History at Grade 1 is national identity told
through people and celebrations; Grade 3 tells the
national story as one continuous people's story —
Lucayans, Columbus, settlers and enslaved Africans,
the freedom holidays and the nation builders — with
African-carried traditions (Junkanoo, asue, Anansi
and Brer tales) as the living thread; Grade 4 tells
a settler-and-nation sequence through named groups
and named people, with surnames taught as historical
evidence; Grade 5 turns to episodes — pirates,
wreckers, blockade runners and bootleggers as the
booms that built the islands, and emancipation
through the freed villages; Grade 6 tells the whole
story as one dated timeline whose spine is the road
to Majority Rule and independence in 1973. The
books' non-History parts (Religious Studies, Health,
Civics, Geography, Science and Economics parts named
in the placements file) stay named in the stocked
sources, not placed.
History lessons: 676 → 689.

All 71 Cyprus placements are stocked: 41 into 10
existing lessons and 30 into the 12 new lessons. The
placed books are the Grade 3 teacher guide
*Διδακτικές προτάσεις και ιδέες για εκπαιδευτικούς που
διδάσκουν Ιστορία — Τάξη Γ΄ Δημοτικού* (cy-g3-proposals,
2013, ISBN 978-9963-0-1391-3, 36 placements), the Grade 3
work cards (cy-g3-cards, 2013, ISBN 978-9963-0-1363-0,
5 placements), the Grade 4 student book *Ο άνθρωπος και
η ιστορία του* (cy-g4-student, 2004, ISBN 9963-0-1253-1,
25 placements) and the Grade 4 work cards (cy-g4-cards,
2013, ISBN 978-9963-0-1411-8, 5 placements). Honesty:
the Grade 3 proposals book is a TEACHER GUIDE — its 36
placements stand as teacher-guide evidence only, counted
nowhere in acceptance. Five of the new lessons stand on
it alone (Palaeolithic minds and cave art, Cyprus
Neolithic — Choirokoitia, Neolithic beliefs and symbols,
Cyprus Bronze Age — copper and the first cities, Bronze
Age — myth and history) and carry acceptance 0, Cyprus
named, counted nowhere; three existing lessons take
Cyprus guide evidence only and stay unchanged (From
evidence to a written history 10, Find history in your
own locality 10, Who studies prehistory 7). Cyprus is
counted where its student books and work cards teach:
What history is 13 → 14, Dating the past 8 → 9,
Comparing states — how kingdoms hold together 4 → 5,
From stone tools to farming 9 → 10, Ancient
civilisations — Greece: Crete 8 → 9, World religions —
the five main faiths 2 → 3, Ancient civilisations —
Rome 9 → 10, and the seven student-built new lessons
carry acceptance 1 (Cyprus). Three Cyprus records are
RECORD ONLY, not placed: the 2016–17 and 2017–18
teaching-media catalogues (wall-map inventories) and
the Agios Dometios school-maps project report — no
History teaching content. No placement slug needed
remapping: every non-new slug exists, and the 12 NEW
LESSON NEEDED slugs were checked against the current
tree — no existing lesson teaches Choirokoitia,
Cyprus's Bronze Age copper cities, its Geometric
city-kingdoms, Phoenicians in Cyprus, Onesilos,
Evagoras or Ptolemaic Cyprus, so the new lessons were
built as the placements direct. Cyprus's own way is
stocked inside the lessons: four enquiry questions
asked of every age, one comparison grid from the
Palaeolithic to the imagined 31st century, and the
island's ancient story told through Hector the boy
archaeologist and Aphrodite, Cyprus never a footnote
to Greece.
History lessons: 712 → 724.

Kuwait's own way is stocked inside the lessons: History
starts from Kuwait and widens — to the Gulf in Grade 6
(with Hamad and his grandfather carrying the founding
story), to the Arab homeland in Grade 7, to the Islamic
world in Grade 8, to the world in Grade 9 — and secondary
History opens with method, then tells Kuwait alone
(roots, rulers, emirate to modern state), Islamic History
as one caliph-by-caliph arc, and world history as a
Europe-outward arc ending at the United Nations. The
books' non-History parts (Geography, Civics/values

sources, not placed.

TEACHER GUIDES, so every Zimbabwe placement is cited as
teacher-guide evidence only, OUTSIDE acceptance counts —
Zimbabwe is named in every stocked lesson but counted
nowhere; no existing lesson's acceptance count changes,
and the 18 new lessons carry acceptance 0. Heritage —
what it is and why it matters already named Zimbabwe as
teacher-guide evidence from the Geography expansion; the
15 History placements there add depth on the same
footing. The Grade 4 guide's garbled answer-key line —
the Bantu "migrated from central Africa in Kenya" — is
kept as printed and flagged in the early-people lesson,
not silently corrected; the ECD B guide's imprint page
misprints the ECD A ISBN and running title (publisher
error), carried as filed. The books' non-History parts
(work and leisure, transport and communication, shelter,
global issues, managing change, etiquette, entitlements,
health, career guidance, religion, social services) stay
named in the stocked sources, not placed. Zimbabwe's own
way is stocked inside the lessons: the Heritage-Based
Curriculum starts History as the child's own story —
name, totem, family, locality — then the nation (anthem,
flag colours as land, minerals, heroes' blood, the
people; the pledge; Independence and Heroes Days), then
heritage split into things you can touch and things you
do and say, carried by Ubuntu/Unhu/Vumunhu; the deep past
enters at Grade 4 (Khoisan, then the Bantu migration),
the pre-colonial states are compared in tables at
Grade 5, and Grade 6 tells the full national narrative in
four acts — colonisation by treaty trickery and the
Pioneer Column, the First Chimurenga, the Second
Chimurenga, and the symbols-and-holidays settlement —
closing at Grade 7 with independence defined and the
land question faced straight.
History lessons: 753 → 771.

Economics among the 17 distinct books) were
cover-verified and filed record-only in the
placements — not placed here. India's own way is
stocked inside the lessons: NCERT teaches history
as a story journey — every chapter opens with a
present-day child whose question starts the chapter,
primary sources are quoted in boxes, an "Elsewhere"
box sets India beside the wider world each chapter,
and kings are taught alongside ordinary people —
farmers, fishers, weavers, slaves — with the book's
title itself plural: Our Pasts, because different
groups lived different pasts.
History lessons: 771 → 815.

the reader. Fiji's own way is stocked inside the
lessons: History begins with the child's own family
tree (Year 3), widens to the village and the national
leaders (Year 4), tells Fiji's arrival story group by
group (Year 5), gives the whole national story as
dated timelines with archive photographs (Year 6),
places Fiji inside the Pacific (Year 7), widens to
the world and brings it back to Fiji examples
(Year 8), proceeds by periods and trades (Year 9),
turns argument-driven (Year 10), and at Year 11–12
becomes themed courses — Cultural Interaction and
Integration, Government and Governance, and
Diplomacy — worked largely from primary sources.

the lessons: Grade 10 opens with a whole book on how

resistance atlas; Grade 12 ends the school story in

(Supersemar, 30 September 1965, May 1998) presented
as evidence problems, not settled slogans.

outside the counts. Three placements were stocked
into the closest existing lessons, their original
slugs recorded in the provenance rows: the Great
Migration and the fall of the West into the
Migration of Peoples — the Huns and the fall of
Rome; the Black Death and late medieval society
into the Black Death in Asia, Europe and Africa;
and Balkan peoples in the Ottoman and Habsburg
empires into the Eastern Question — nationalism
in the Balkans. Every other stocked lesson adds
Montenegro to its countries line and its
acceptance count together. Montenegro's own way
is stocked inside the lessons: Grade 6 ends
antiquity on home ground in Doclea; Grade 7
alternates Europe and Duklja/Zeta; Grade 8
alternates Europe's revolutions with Montenegro
1697–1914 building its state; Grade 9 tells the
twentieth century as a double world-and-
Montenegro/Yugoslavia story ending with the
renewal of independence.

HONESTY: all three placed books are student books, so every placement counts; no teacher guides in this set. The placements file's 6 records are 3 distinct books — each Riva textbook appears twice in the targets file (once as a release-asset record and once as a books.json duplicate) and is counted once, as filed. 19 placements whose named slug does not exist in the current tree are stocked into the closest existing lesson covering that thing, with the original slug recorded in each provenance row: the barbarian invasions and the Vikings and the Great Migration into The Migration of Peoples — the Huns and the fall of Rome; Charlemagne's Carolingian Empire and the feudal kingdoms of France, England and the Holy Roman Empire into Medieval world — states and empires; medieval Christianity spreads into Medieval Europe — Church, towns and Joan of Arc; knights, castles and the feudal pyramid into Medieval world — the Dark Age and feudalism; life in a medieval village and town and cities, guilds and fairs revive into Medieval cities, trade, universities and plague; the Church and medieval culture and the Gothic cathedrals into Gothic art — cathedrals and towns; the Crusades — why Europeans went into The Crusades — two centuries for Jerusalem; oral history — legends and fairy tales into From evidence to a written history; the Ottoman Empire — rise and fall into Medieval world — the Ottomans; the first tools and fire into Hominisation — the first humans; the Neolithic Revolution into From stone tools to farming — the Neolithic Revolution. One placement named NEW, ancient-world/arab-civilization-and-the-rise-of-islam, is stocked into the existing lesson The rise and expansion of Islam, which already covers the Arabs' unification under Islam and the civilisation built from Arabia to Spain, with the original slug recorded in its provenance row. Stories and readings are stocked as plot summaries only, never transcribed.
